Each spell counts as a sorcerer spell for you, but it doesn’t count against the number of sorcerer spells you know.  Whenever you gain a sorcerer level, you can replace one spell you gained from this feature with another spell of the same level. The new spell must be an illusion or a necromancy spell from the sorcerer, warlock, or wizard spell list.  Shadow Spells Level        Spells 1st            Cause Fear, Ray of Sickness 3rd        Pass Without Trace, Shadow Blade 5th        summon shadowspawn, vampiric touch 7th        phantasmal killer, shadow of moil 9th        creation, enervation
	Stone (UA)									A Stone sorcerer can use the following optional class feature:  Level 1: Metal Magic You learn additional spells when you reach certain levels in this class, as shown on the Stone Spells table. Each of these spells counts as a sorcerer spell for you, but it doesn't count against the number of sorcerer spells you know.  Whenever you gain a sorcerer level, you can replace one spell you gained from this feature with another spell of the same level. The new spell must be an evocation or transmutation spell from the sorcerer, warlock, or wizard spell list.  Stone Spells Level        Spells 1st            Searing Smite, Thunderous Smite 3rd           Branding Smite, Magic Weapon 5th           Blinding Smite, Elemental Weapon 7th           Staggering Smite, Stoneskin 9th           Banishing Smite, Steel Wind Strike

Warlock (PHB 2014)	(choose one)	d8	Requires CHA 13  Light armor, simple weapons.  If you have both the spellcasting class feature and the Pact Magic class feature from the warlock class, you can use the spell slots you gain from the Pact Magic feature to cast spells you know or have prepared from classes with the spellcasting class feature, and you can use the spell slots you gain from the spellcasting class feature to cast warlock spells you know.	Additional Warlock Spells Pact Boon: Pact of the Talisman Eldritch Versatility Eldritch Invocation Options						- A Mystic Arcanum can be swapped for another each time a level 11+ Warlock gains a level in this class, not just when gaining an ASI or feat. - A warlock with Pact of the Blade can use their Charisma for attack and damage rolls for their pact weapon. - The talisman of Pact of the Talisman can't be sold and traded. Only other creatures in the same adventure as the warlock can benefit from the talisman. - Any spells you can cast as a result of a Warlock class feature (such as an Eldritch Invocation) are warlock spells for you. - Pact of the Chain has the following additional Find Familiar options: Abyssal Chicken, Anvilwrought Raptor, Cranium Rat, Crawling Claw, Flying Monkey, Gazer, Imp, Pseudodragon, Quasit, Slime Familiar, Sprite, Tressym, Dust Mephit, Ice Mephit, Magma Mephit, Mud Mephit, Smoke Mephit, Steam Mephit
	Archfey (PHB 2014)

	Fiend (PHB 2014)
	Genie (TCE)									A Genie's Vessel can safely contain a bag of holding or other magic items which create extradimensional spaces, or vice versa.  Using Limited Wish in downtime to achieving a lasting effect costs DTP = the level of the spell produced and you have to expend 1d4 DTP before you can use it again. The following also holds true for Limited Wish: - Limited Wish requires you to have your Genie's Vessel worn or carried and the spell produced uses your warlock spell save DC, spell attack bonus, and spellcasting ability. - Limited Wish produces the effect of a spell instead of you casting it. If the produced spell requires concentration, it instead lasts for its full duration. If the produced spell has a range of self, you're the target. If the produced spell has a range of self with an area, you're the point of origin. Targets of the produced spell must be in the spell's range of you and there must be a clear path from you to the targets. - For purposes of all other effects written in the produced spell, you're treated as the spell's caster.

# Backgrounds

Backgrounds
Characters on the server can originate from the Forgotten Realms or broader D&D 5e multiverse, which includes the different D&D planes of existence and other worlds of the Material Plane corresponding to campaign settings that have official or approved D&D 5e source material (e.g. Eberron, Dragonlance, Greyhawk, Humblewood, etc). Originating from another world or setting requires a request to the Lore Consultants.

Harpspeak	—	Harpers	Harpers, one of the five factions of Faerûn					- This language can only ever be learned as a Stalwart Renown Perk (Renown Score 10+) as part of the Harpers in a session  - Harpspeak is a secret non-verbal language and means of communication known only to Harpers. Harpspeak can be used either in the form of a sign language or with scribed runes to leave hidden messages.   As a sign language, Harpspeak uses ordinary gestures in patterns that allow for messages to be hidden in seemingly normal and innocuous gestures such as smiles, frowns, and winks. Only a creature that knows Harpspeak understands such messages. It takes four times longer to convey such a message than it does to speak the same idea plainly.   As scribed runes, Harpspeak can be used to leave behind hidden messages for other Harpers. Creatures who know Harpspeak automatically spot such messages. Others spot the message's presence with a successful DC 15 Wisdom (Perception) check but can't decipher it without magic. Harpers sometimes mark the interior of these runes with extra dots to leave behind a misleading message for their enemies and to alert their fellow Harpers that the true symbol and its meaning is somewhere else nearby.
Monster Languages
Many monster languages are difficult to speak or understand owing to physiology or means of communication unique to that species of monster and many have no written form
Monster languages can't be learned in downtime except as taught by a PC that knows the language or as an adventure reward, using the gold and DTP cost for learning an Exotic / Rare language

# Spells

Spell	Source	Notes	Rage Advice
Spells from PHB (2014)	Player's Handbook (2014)		- Awaken: can't be used by PCs to increase their Intelligence or gain languages. - Clone: A creature can have only 1 existing clone, with both caster and target spending 128 DTP (if you are both the caster and target, it is 128 DTP total). - Contingency: can't be stored in a Glyph of Warding. - Fabricate: The raw materials can be bought in downtime and cost the full cost of the item. - Find Familiar: In addition to the listed options, the chosen familiar can also be any Beast that is CR 0, as well as the following options: Blood Hawk, Flying Snake. - Find Steed: The chosen steed can be any Medium or larger Beast in Fair Game that is CR 1/2 or less. - Gate: A creature named by the spell knows it is being targeted by the spell and can choose to refuse being summoned, causing the spell to fail. - Glyph of Warding: can't be cast into the extradimensional space of a bag of holding or other magic items that create extradimensional spaces. - Magic Jar: can't be used to permanently swap bodies - Message: doesn't use verbal components - Planar Binding: A PC can only have 1 existing bound creature. - Reincarnate: The body part used in the spell must be taken from after the target has died and can include blood. A reincarnated PC can swap any racial feats they no longer qualify for with another feat or ASI. A PC reincarnated into a variant human gains a starting feat. A PC reincarnated out of variant human loses their starting feat.  - Simulacrum: Only 1 simulacrum can exist per caster, and a simulacrum itself cannot cast this spell. The simulacrum can only regain HP by the alchemical process. - Symbol: can't be cast into the extradimensional space of a bag of holding or other magic items that create extradimensional spaces. - Teleportation Circle: PCs know the following sigil sequences: -----, Durlag's Tower, Lerwick Outpost, Merkin's Keep, Port Nyanzaru, Suzail, Silent Tower, Tinkerton - Teleport: can't be used in downtime except to a permanent circle or a safe location you possess an associated object for - True Polymorph: Any permanent transformations are reverted at the end of a DM's adventure, except a a PC transformed as a type of character retirement. - Wish: Using the spell beyond the scope of duplicating a spell or the bulleted options requires approval from the Lore Consultants and/or Rule Architects, unless the effect is limited to the scope of a DM's adventure. If you receive the effect of a Wish from other than a PC or a PC-controlled creature, its effect is limited to the scope of a DM's adventure except as approved by the Lore Consultants and/or Rule Architects. A character that loses the ability to cast the spell can never regain the ability to do so, even by reworking, except only by rerolling the stress roll with another Wish spell as per the spell's written ability to reroll any roll made within 1 round. A PC-controlled creature casting this spell (such as a simulacrum) can only use it to duplicate a spell as written. Additionally, a creature that gains resistance to a damage type from this spell only benefits from one instance at a time; if the spell is cast again to confer damage resistance, only the most recent casting applies.
Spells from SCAG	Sword Coast Adventurer's Guide		- Booming Blade: The cost requirement for the weapon is removed, the spell's range is the weapon's reach (not Self), and the spell targets a creature within the weapon's reach. The spell can be used with War Caster. - Green-Flame Blade: The cost requirement for the weapon is removed, the spell's range is the weapon's reach (not Self), and the spell targets a creature within the weapon's reach. The spell can be used with War Caster. - Lightning Lure: The spell's range is 15 feet (not Self) and targets all other creatures in range. The spell can be used with War Caster. - Sword Burst: The spell's range is 5 feet (not Self) and targets all other creatures in range. The spell can be used with War Caster.
Spells from XGTE	Xanathar's Guide to Everything	All Wizard spells are present in the ----- Community Spellbook	Find Greater Steed: In addition to the listed options, the chosen steed can be any Beast that is CR 2 or less, as well as the following options: Dragonnel, Hippogriff, Skyjek Roc, Star Lancer
Spells from GGR	Guildmaster's Guide to Ravnica	Encode thoughts is Wizard only	Encode thoughts: Creating a thought strand doesn't cause the creature to lose memory of the associated thought or memory.
Spells from LLK	Lost Laboratory of Kwalish	All Wizard spells are present in the ----- Community Spellbook	- Flock of Familiars: Doesn't require concentration and the spell ends early if you dismiss the familiars with an action or cast the spell again. A Pact of the Chain warlock can't summon Pact of the Chain familiars with the spell. - Galder's Speedy Courier: After receiving a chest, the target can block your ability to reach it again with this spell for 8 hours. If you try to send another chest during that time, you learn you are blocked, and the spell fails.

The spell can be used with War Caster. - Dream of the Blue Veil: The material component can't be substitued with a component pouch or spellcasting focus. - Green-Flame Blade: The cost requirement for the weapon is removed, the spell's range is the weapon's reach (not Self), and the spell targets a creature within the weapon's reach. The spell can be used with War Caster. - Lightning Lure: The spell's range is 15 feet (not Self) and targets all other creatures in range. Spells from PHB (2024)	Player's Handbook (2024)	Can only be used by 2024 characters.  - Befuddlement replaces Feeblemind from PHB 2014. - Find Steed also supersedes Find Greater Steed from XGTE. - Shining Smite replaces Branding Smite from PHB 2014. - Summon Dragon is the renamed version of Summon Draconic Spirit from FTD, but a 2024 character can use either version of the spell.  Refer to the PHB 2024 Spell Changes for more details.  All Wizard spells are present in the ----- Community Spellbook	- Awaken: can't be used by PCs to increase their Intelligence or gain languages. - Clone: A creature can have only 1 existing clone, with both caster and target spending 128 DTP (if you are both the caster and target, it is 128 DTP total). - Contingency: can't be stored into a Glyph of Warding. - Fabricate: The raw materials can be bought in downtime and cost the full cost of the item. - Find Familiar: In addition to the listed options, the following options can be used: Blood Hawk, Flying Snake - Gate: A creature named by the spell knows it is being targeted by the spell and can choose to refuse being summoned, causing the spell to fail. - Glyph of Warding: can't be cast into the extradimensional space of a bag of holding or other magic items that create extradimensional spaces. - Magic Jar: can't be used to permanently swap bodies. - Planar Binding: A PC can only have 1 existing bound creature. - Reincarnate: The body part used in the spell must be taken from after the target has died and can include blood. A reincarnated PC can swap any racial feats they no longer qualify for with another feat or ASI. A PC reincarnated into a human gains an additional Origin feat. A PC reincarnated out of human loses their racial Origin feat.  - Simulacrum: Only 1 simulacrum can exist per caster. - Symbol: can't be cast into the extradimensional space of a bag of holding or other magic items that create extradimensional spaces. - Animate Objects, Giant Insect, Summon Aberration / Beast / Celestial / Construct / Dragon / Elemental / Fey / Fiend / Undead: The summoned creature has a number of Hit Dice equal to the spell level (d6 HD for Small, d8 HD for Medium, and d10 HD for Large) - Tasha's Bubbling Cauldron: creates the caster's choice of a number of T1 or lower potions.These potions can't be traded. - Teleportation Circle: PCs know the following sigil sequences: -----, Durlag's Tower, Lerwick Outpost, Merkin's Keep, Port Nyanzaru, Suzail, Silent Tower, Tinkerton - Teleport: can't be used in downtime except to a permanent circle or a safe location you possess a linked object for - True Polymorph: Any permanent transformations are reverted at the end of a DM's adventure, except a a PC transformed as a type of character retirement. - Wish: Using the spell to Reshape Reality requires approval from the Lore Consultants and/or Rule Architects, unless the effect is limited to the scope of a DM's adventure. If you receive the effect of a Wish from other than a PC or a PC-controlled creature, its effect is limited to the scope of a DM's adventure except as approved by the Lore Consultants and/or Rule Architects. A character that loses the ability to cast the spell can never regain the ability to do so, even by reworking, except only by Roll Redo from another Wish spell or Heroic Inspiration. A PC-controlled creature casting this spell (such as a simulacrum) can only use it to duplicate a spell as written. Additionally, a creature only benefits from one instance of the Resistance option at a time; if the spell is used again to confer Resistance, only the most recent casting applies.
Spells from FRHOF (2024)	Forgotten Realms: Heroes of Faerûn (2024)	Can only be used by 2024 characters.  Circle Magic can be used as written with any eligible spell in Allowed Content (Spells)  Deryan's Helpful Homunculi and Conjure Constructs are also Artificer spells for the 2024 EFOTA Artificer  All Wizard spells except the following are present in the ----- Community Spellbook: Backlash, Conjure Constructs, and Spellfire Storm	- Deryan's Helpful Homunculi: The assistant created by this spell can assist with crafting either Equipment or in crafting magic items. If the assistant is used to help craft Equipment, the DTP cost to craft that Equipment is halved (rounded up). However, you must pay for the spell’s Material component costs for each DTP spent this way to craft and additionally add the spell level this spell is cast at in DTP. For crafting magic items, the assistant cannot be used to assist in crafting any magic items that can be used to cast spells or that have restricted attunement requirements. The assistant otherwise can be used to halve the base DTP cost to craft a magic item (rounded up) at your Bastion as if they were an assisting PC. However, you must pay for the spell’s Material component costs for each DTP spent this way to craft and additionally add the spell level this spell is cast at in DTP, as well as the additional 3 or 21 DTP as normal. The assistant can be used in conjunction with an assisting PC. The DTP cost to craft a magic item in this way is two-thirds of its original base DTP cost, with you and the assisting PC expending one-third of the base DTP cost each (round up). However, you must pay for the spell’s Material component costs for each DTP you spent this way to craft and additionally add the spell level this spell is cast at in DTP, as well as the additional 3 or 21 DTP as normal. - Dirge: The Speed of a target that succeeds on its saving throw is halved until the end of the creature's next turn. - Doomtide: The spell’s special component (a string of three black pearls from Pandemonium) that is used when casting the spell as a circle spell can’t be obtained in downtime but instead typically from an adventure.

Mounts, Vehicles, and Pets
Mounts, Vehicles, and Pets can be resold for half price.
Jump to
Mounts	Vehicles	Pets
Mounts (Go to Mounts, Vehicles, and Pets)
A mount's participation in an adventure is at the DM's discretion.
A DM can use the guidelines on customizing monsters in the DM Guidelines for mounts they award.
A DM can choose to award a mount that has been awakened (such as by the Awaken spell) but the pet is then considered to be a sapient mount.
A sapient mount's name, alignment, and personality must be written in the session log in which it is acquired.
Sapient mounts cannot be traded or sold.

Pets (Go to Mounts, Vehicles, and Pets)
Pets can't take the Attack or Help actions and normally can't participate in adventures: a pet only participates in adventures otherwise with a DM's express permission and at their discretion
A DM can submit a Rules request to award a creature not listed here as a vanity pet
Pets can also be awarded as the equivalent of a T0 Permanent item and follow the associated item distribution rules for doing so 
A DM can use the guidelines on customizing monsters in the DM Guidelines for pets they award.
Sapient pets cannot be traded or sold.                                                
A sapient pet's name, alignment, and personality must be written in the session log in which it is acquired.
A DM can choose to award a pet that has been awakened (such as by the Awaken spell) but the pet is then considered to be a sapient pet.

# Downtime

You can't spent the same DTP for more than one activity. Except where otherwise stated, the minimum DTP for any activity is 1.
You additionally expend 1 GP per DTP expended on activities other than Traveling while not in -----

Bastions
You must be level 5+ to build and benefit from a Bastion. You can only build and benefit from one Bastion as part of downtime (see Appendix B of the TBD Guidelines for details)
You can only construct a Bastion either a) near ----- or a guild outpost or b) at another world location if you are able to claim a parcel of land or stronghold as part of an adventure as determined by the DM.
Name of Activity		Gold (- means gold spent)	DTP Cost	Description					Notes / Rage Advice
Construct or Expand a Bastion		Varies	Varies	Constructing or expanding a Bastion by adding or replacing one or more facilities. See the Bastions sheet for details and Appendix B of the TBD Guidelines for details.					- Bastions use the rules for Bastions as described in chapter 8 of the 2024 Dungeon Master's Guide, with modifications as listed in the Bastions sheet and Appendix B of the TBD Guidelines. - Your starting Bastion must contain two Basic Facilities (one Cramped, one Roomy) and two Special Facilities you qualify for. - Any facility or component built as part of a Bastion can be torn down and 50% of its gold cost refunded. The Bastion must still contain at least two Basic Facilities (including one Cramped and one Roomy) and two Special Facilities afterward to be useable.                                                                                                                 - A Special Facility can be replaced with another Special Facility by paying the gold and DTP to build the new facility, but the full gold cost of the old facility contributes to the new one                                                                                                                 - You can combine your Bastion with Bastions built by one or more characters of other players into a single structure as written in the 2024 Dungeon Master's Guide (page 334). Each participant can mutually contribute gold and DTP in building and expanding Bastions in this way.                                                                                                                
Construct or Expand a Shared Bastion		Varies	Varies	Constructing or expanding a Bastion that is Shared by characters of different players.* See the Bastions sheet for details and Appendix B of the TBD Guidelines for details.**  * Each participant must build one or more Special Facilities of a Shared Bastion, with the maximum number of Special Facilities in a Shared Bastion according to the highest level participating PC (up to 6 by level 17+).  ** An existing Bastion can be converted into a Shared Bastion with new participants constructing one or more Special Facilities. If the Bastion already has the maximum number of Special Facilities according to the highest level participating PC, an existing Special Facility must be torn down first to make room. Additionally, Shared Bastions can be Combined with either the singular or Shared Bastions of PCs of other players as normal.					- A Shared Bastion is a single Bastion built and expanded by one or more characters of different players. This Bastion counts as the single Bastion a character can benefit from at a single time and each participant can mutually benefit from and issue orders to any Special Facility they meet the prerequisites for. - To construct a Shared Bastion, the construction must be specifically logged as a Shared Bastion in #downtime-logs, with each participant logging the facilities they are contributing gold and DTP to build. A participant must contribute the entire gold and DTP cost to construct at least one Special Facility of a Shared Bastion to benefit from a Shared Bastion. Likewise, the entire cost of any individual Basic Facility or other component (such as a Defensive Wall) must be fully paid for in gold and DTP by a single participant at a time. - The initial construction logs by each participant must also indicate the initial list of Special Facilities each participant is choosing to benefit from. The maximum number of Special Facilities any individual participant can benefit from at a time still depends on their character level as per the table in Appendix B of the TBD Guidelines and the maximum number of such facilities in the Bastion depends on the highest level participant. - When expanding a Shared Bastion with additional Basic and Special Facilities, each participant must also log which newly constructed Special Facilities if any they are now benefitting from. These logs must reply to the most recent construction or expansion logs in #downtime-logs and must have an updated list of the Bastion's facilities. - A participant that levels up and can benefit from more Special Facilities must likewise log which additional Special Facilities if any they are choosing to gain the benefit of and must reply to the most recent construction or expansion log in #downtime-logs - Besides adding to the list of Special Facilities they are benefitting from, a participant can swap one Special Facility they are benefitting from for another existing Special Facility by expending the DTP cost to construct the latter Special Facility and log the swap in #downtime-logs by replying to the most recent construction or expansion log in #downtime-logs - If a participant wishes to tear down a facility or component or otherwise replace a Special Facility with another one, they must log doing so in #downtime-logs by replying to the most recent construction or expansion log and with an updated list of the Bastion's facilities. A participant can only tear down or replace a facility or component they constructed. Each participant must log which Special Facilities if any they are now benefitting from, excising any Special Facility that was torn down from their list. - Bastion Turns are issued individually as if a participant was the sole owner of the Bastion and can only order facilities they are benefitting from.                                                                                                       
Taking a Bastion Turn		0.0	7.0	Taking a Bastion Turn and issuing orders to one or more of your Bastion's Special Facilities (except Maintain)					- When taking a Bastion Turn in downtime, you can't issue the Maintain order. You must be present at your Bastion or have a way to communicate with your Bastion's hirelings (e.g. Sending) to take a Bastion turn. (Because the Maintain order can't be issued, Bastion Events can't occur during downtime) - When taking a Bastion Turn during an adventure, you must still expend 7 DTP unless the Maintain order is issued. A DM can choose to incorporate Bastion Events during an adventure after the Maintain order has been issued. Any rewards obtained via a Bastion Event must fit within the adventure's gold and item allotment using the server rules.

Scribing Spell Scroll (Cantrip)		-15	1.0	Scribing a cantrip spell scroll. See PHB 2024 for additional information.					- To scribe a spell scroll, as per the 2024 PHB, you must know or have the spell prepared, you must have proficiency with Arcana or Calligrapher's Supplies, you must be able to cast the spell, and you must provide the spell's material components. If the material components are normally consumed, they are consumed when the scroll is completed. - A scribed spell scroll uses your spell attack bonus, spell save DC, and spellcasting ability modifier. - A scribed cantrip spell scroll works as if the caster were your level. - A scribed level 2+ spell scroll can be an upcast version of a lower level spell you know or have prepared. - The maximum number of scribed spell scrolls you can have during a session is equal to your Proficiency Bonus. Must provide this information to the DM prior to start of session. - You can't sell or trade any spell scrolls you scribe from this downtime activity.

Crafting T0 Consumable		-25*	3**	Crafting a T0 Consumable in Rolled or Select Loot.					Crafting magic items is based on the rules of the 2024 Dungeon Master's Guide with the following changes: - Crafting a magic item of a certain type requires requires proficiency in the Arcana skill, proficiency in a corresponding tool, and an associated Special Facility of a Bastion depending on the type of item. The required tool proficiency is based on the Magic Item Tools table in chapter 7 of the 2024 Dungeon Master's Guide and is individually listed for each item in Loot. The required Special Facility is detailed in the Bastions sheet. - You can only craft magic items of your character's tier or lower.                                                                                                             - If a magic item can be used to cast spells, the spells must be among your Spells Prepared or Spells Known in order to craft the item, you must be able to cast the spell, and you must provide the material components for each spell. If the material components are normally consumed, they are consumed for each spell when the item is complete.                                                                                                                 - If a magic item requires attunement, you must be able to attune to it in order to craft the item. - Another player's PC can provide assistance in crafting, with each participating PC expending half the base DTP cost (round up). A PC can only assist in this way if they are in the same location and are individually capable of crafting the item (in terms of Arcana and tool proficiency, spells known or prepared, and attunement). - A Base Cost and associated Crafting Cost is listed for craftable items: the Crafting Cost is what must be expended to craft the item. The Base Cost does not mean that the magic item can be purchased.                                                                                                     * Certain magic items can cost more than this value to craft: be sure to check an item's notes in Loot. Additionally, if a magic item incorporates an item with a purchase cost (such as magic armor), you must pay its full cost or craft that item in order to craft the associated magic item. For example magic plate armor would require paying an additional 1500 GP or an additional 750 GP and 60 DTP and craft the armor.              ** Certain magic items can cost more DTP than this value to craft: be sure to check an item's notes in Loot. Additionally, if a magic item is crafted in ----- or a city (population >5000), an additional 3 DTP must be expended after any potential reductions. If it is crafted elsewhere, an additional 21 DTP must be expended instead after any potential reductions. Every magic item costs an additional 3 or 21 DTP to craft as a result.

Reworking
All reworks must be logged in #character-rework-log for approval by an Auditor
You must log all reworks in your Master Adventurer Log (MAL) in the session section
You can fully refund any rework prior to your first game played or DMPCed with that character. Log the refund in #character-rework-log for approval by an Auditor
You can partially refund (50% GP and DTP recouped) any rework before your 3rd game played or DMPCed with that character. Log the refund in #character-rework-log for approval by an Auditor

(The previous character becomes retired as an NPC but the same MAL sheet is used). - A character is defined as existing from August 2025 and prior if they are mentioned in at least one log in the #session-log, #downtime-logs, or #trade-logs from August 2025 and before

(The previous character becomes retired as an NPC but the same MAL sheet is used) - A character NPCed in this way can be restored with another checkpoint rework provided that MAL sheet wasn't used as part of a consolidation  - A character reworked into another character this way retains all equipment and magic items, but doesn't retain any supernatural rewards, sapient pets, or sapient mounts. Any Relics of Emergence or Hoard Magic Items they own revert to their initial state.

Ala Carte Rework (Level 6)		-160	30.0	Single change rework for level 6 PC					The listed cost is per change: - Changing 1 class level, your race / species (including racial features and ASIs), background (including features,  proficiencies, and/or ASIs) and changing a feat or ASI  all cost 1 change each - Changing a language known or tool or skill proficiency independent of other changes costs 1 change - Changing a subclass costs 1 change per class level that adds a subclass feature. (You aren't charged for a subclass change added as a result of a class level change) - Changing starting ability scores, or changing the character to a different person, costs 2 changes - Changing from 2014 to 2024 costs 1 change. You must change your species, class, subclass, feats, and all other character options to their 2024 equivalent unless that option has not been reprinted. Your starting ASIs from your species are replaced with ASIs of your choice from your background, which can be customized as written in Appendix A of the TBD Guidelines. - Changing from 2024 to 2014 costs 1 change. You must change your species, class, subclass, feats, and all other character options to their 2014 equivalent. If there is no 2014 equivalent for a character option, you must choose an eligible 2014 option instead. This does not incur any additional costs.

Name of Activity		Gold (- means gold spent)	DTP Cost	Description					Notes / Rage Advice
Casting a Spell with a lasting effect		Varies	Level of the spell (minimum 1)	Casting a spell to achieve a lasting effect, such as Simulacrum					- You must expend any costly material components for the spell that are consumed (if any) as the gold cost for this activity - If the effect has a finite duration of 1 day or longer, only in character days that pass as part of an adventure count against the duration as determined by the DM. - If another spell is cast as part of casting a spell with a lasting effect (such as the Contingency or Glyph of Warding spell), only DTP equal to the highest spell level used needs to be expended. For example, casting a level 6 Contingency spell with a level 4 Otiluke's Resilient Sphere spell in downtime costs only 6 DTP.

Copying a Spell		-50 GP (per spell level)	Special	Copying a spell into your spellbook at a rate of 2 hours per spell level. You expend 1 DTP for every 8 hours that pass this way (round up, minimum 1 DTP), allowing you to copy multiple spells per DTP expended.					- While in -----, you can copy spells from the Community Spellbook. Refer to the listed Spells in this document. - You can copy a spell from another PC's spellbook, but both you and that PC must expend the DTP
Copying your Spellbook or a Prepared Spell		-10 GP (per spell level)	Special	Copying a spell from your spellbook or a spell you have prepared into another book at a rate of 1 hour per spell level. You expend 1 DTP for every 8 hours that pass this way (round up, minimum 1 DTP), allowing you to copy multiple spells per DTP expended.					- While in -----, you can copy spells into the Community Spellbook. Refer to the listed Spells in this document.

These spellcasting services by NPCs can only be used in ----- or in other villages (level 0 - 2 spells) or other towns or cities (level 0 - 5 spells)

Trading
Only PCs in the same location can trade with each other and any items you obtain via trading with another PC must be of your PC's tier or lower
You can't trade gold or equipment that can be sold for gold between your PCs, even by proxy
Items and equpment created by class features can't be traded or sold. Supernatural Rewards also cannot be traded or sold.
Name of Activity		Gold (- means gold spent)	DTP Cost	Description					Notes / Rage Advice
Trading Gold		Varies	0.0	You give or receive an amount of gold to or from another player's character					- To avoid self trade of gold by proxy, the log must explain for what reason the gold or equipment was given e.g. given for the purpose of retraining. - After an item is traded between two PCs, those two PCs can't re-trade the item between them again until after 14 days have passed
Trading Equipment		Varies	0.0	You give or receive equipment to or from another player's character
Trading Magic Items		Varies	0.0	You give or receive a magic item to or from another player's character					- Any items you obtain via trading must be of your PC's tier or lower. The only exception are items obtained as part of a "last will," but you still cannot use an item above your PC's tier whether during downtime or an adventure. - After an item is traded between two PCs, those two PCs can't re-trade the item between them again until after 14 days have passed
Self Trading		0.0	0.0	You trade an item from one of your PCs to another one of your PCs					- You can't trade a magic item obtained on one of your PCs to another one of your PCs until after 30 days have passed since the date of the session or downtime log where you obtained the item. You can't retrade that item back between the PCs until after another 30 days have passed. - You can't trade gold or items that can be sold for gold between your PCs, even via proxy. A PR investigation will be opened if you and other parties are suspected of violating this rule.
Loaning an Item		Varies	0.0	You give or receive an item to or from another player's character as a loan for a minimum period of 14 or more days					The transaction must be logged as a loan and must last a minimum of 14 days.
Training
Besides learning by yourself, another player's PC that knows a language or has a tool proficiency can teach your PC that language or tool proficiency. You expend the listed DTP cost but no gold and the teacher PC expends half the listed DTP.
Both your PC and the teacher PC must be in the same location, and the teacher must be another player's PC; the teacher cannot be one of your own PCs.
The teacher can choose at their discretion to charge you gold for teaching, using the rules for trading gold.

Learning an Exotic / Rare Language		-250	100.0	Learning an Exotic / Rare language. See link to Languages sheet for details.					- Celestial, Infernal, and Vedalken can be learned in ----- or other major cities - Other Exotic / Rare languages can't be learned in ----- but can be in Waterdeep or other major cities
Learning a Monster Language		-250	100.0	Learning a Monster language. See link to Languages sheet for details.					- Can't be learned in downtime except as an adventure reward or as taught by another PC - Many monster languages are difficult to speak or understand owing to physiology or means of communication unique to that species of monster and many have no written form
Learning an Ethnic Language		-250	100.0	Learning an Ethnic language. See link to Languages sheet for details.					Learning in downtime requires traveling to one of the associated listed regions

Gaining a Gaming Set Proficiency		-120	60.0	Gaining proficiency with a type of Gaming Set					Requires possessing the associated gaming set
Gaining Disguise Kit, Forgery Kit, or Thieves' Tools Proficiency		-500	100.0	Gaining proficiency with a Disguise Kit, Forgery Kit, or Thieves' Tools					Requires possessing the associated tool

Traveling
Traveling costs 1 DTP and 1 GP per day of travel and uses the travel rules in the PHB (2014 p.181, 2024 p. 20) and DMG (2014 p. 242), with a maximum of 8 hours traveled per day.
Travel distances to major cities are in the TBD Guidelines. Contact a Lore Consultant in #downtime-planning to help determine distances and plan travel as necessary.
Name of Activity		Gold (- means gold spent)	DTP Cost	Description					Notes / Rage Advice
Traveling on land		-1 GP per day of travel	1 DTP per day of travel	Traveling to a destination on land via walking, a mount, or a land vehicle					- Use the distance via roads to determine the number of days traveled - A PC on foot, riding a land vehicle, or on a typical mount covers 24 miles in a day - A PC on a Phantom Steed can cover 10 miles in 1 hour (or 80 miles in a day if the PC can keep the spell up the entire day)
Traveling by ship passage		-1 SP per mile traveled and either -1.5 GP (hammock) or -3 GP (private cabin) per day of travel	1 DTP per day of travel	Traveling from one port to another via ship as a passenger, resting either in a hammock or private cabin					- Use the distance between ports to determine the number of days traveled - A typical sailing ship covers 48 miles in a day to travel along the sea - A keelboat covers 24 miles in a day to travel along a river 
Traveling via flight		-1 GP per day of travel	1 DTP per day of travel	Traveling to a destination via a flying mount or magical flight					- Use the straightline distance between locations to determine the number of days traveled - For flying via a flying mount or a magical item that grants sustained flight, you can travel up to 8 times the hourly rate of the source of flight in a day regardless of if it is a flying mount or magical item that grants sustained flight - A PC under the effects of Wind Walk can cover 240 miles in a day
Traveling via owned air, space, or water vehicle		-1 GP per day of travel and -2 GP per crew member per day of travel	1 DTP per day of travel	Traveling to a destination via an air, space, or water vehicle you own					- A vehicle can cover 24 times its listed hourly speed in a day - A water vehicle can only travel from one port to another port - An air vehicle can be landed safely on the ground - A canoe, keelboat, or rowboat can only travel via a river - Certain space vehicles can only be landed safely on the ground or in water (such as a port), not both - Use the straightline distance between locations to determine the number of days traveled via an air or space vehicle - Travel from Toril to the Rock of Bral or vice versa via a space vehicle takes 1 DTP for 1 day of travel (2 DTP for 2 days for a round trip) - Vehicles with a listed amount of Crew require that number of skilled hirelings for each day of use, costing 2 GP per hireling (included in Gold Spent) - Other characters (up to the vehicle's Passenger limit) can also travel with you in this way, but also expend gold and DTP to travel, though they do not have to pay for Crew costs
Traveling via a teleportation spell		Varies	Level of the spell (minimum 1)	Traveling to a destination via a teleportation spell					- The caster must expend any costly material components for the spell that are consumed (if any) as the gold cost for this activity - The caster and any other characters that are teleported must expend DTP equal to the level of the spell used - You can't use Teleport in downtime except to a permanent circle or a safe location you possess an associated / linked object for - All PCs know the following sigil sequences: Durlag's Tower, Lerwick Outpost, Merkin's Keep (near Baldur's Gate), Port Nyanzaru (in the Temple of Savras), Suzail, Silent Tower (Mompono Village, Chult), Tinkerton (near Waterdeep)
Traveling from ----- to the Rock of Bral or vice versa		-15 GP	1 DTP	Traveling to the Rock of Bral from ----- (or vice versa) via spelljamming transport provided by New Nivix					A round trip costs 30 GP and 2 DTP
Travelling into or out of an extradimensional space		Varies	Varies	Travelling into or out from an extradimensional space you have access to via a spell or magic item (e.g. Demiplane)					- For a spell, the caster must expend any costly material components for the spell that are consumed (if any) as the gold cost for this activity - If a spell is used to travel into or out from an extradimensional space you have access to, the DTP cost is equal to the level of spell - If a magic item is used to travel into or out from an extradimensional space you have access to without casting a spell (e.g. Rod of Security), the DTP cost is 1
Traveling beyond Faerûn		Varies	Level of the spell (minimum 1)	Traveling via a teleportation spell to a known safe location beyond Faerûn. 					- Locations beyond Faerûn include other continents on Toril, Wildspace, other planes of existence, and other worlds - Outside of teleporting to a known safe location (such as to a known teleportation circle), travel to locations beyond Faerûn can only occur as part of an adventure or as otherwise overseen by a DM - The caster must expend any costly material components for the spell that are consumed (if any) as the gold cost for this activity - The caster and any other characters that are teleported must expend DTP equal to the level of the spell used
Work

# Bastions

Jump to
Basic Facilities		Special Facilities		Other
A starting Bastion must contain two Basic Facilities (one Cramped and one Roomy) and two Special Facilities. You must be level 5+ and meet all other prerequisites. See Appendix B of the TBD Guidelines for details.
Any facility or component built as part of a Bastion can be torn down and 50% of its gold cost refunded. The Bastion must still contain at least two Basic Facilities (including one Cramped and one Roomy) and two Special Facilities afterward to be useable.
A Special Facility can be replaced with another Special Facility by paying the gold and DTP to build the new facility, but the full gold cost of the old facility contributes to the new one
You can combine your Bastion with Bastions built by one or more characters of other players into a single structure as written in the 2024 Dungeon Master's Guide.  Each participant can mutually contribute gold and DTP in building and expanding Bastions in this way.
Any items acquired or crafted from Bastions are subject to the normal trading rules (including needing to wait 30 days before self trading)

				Prerequisite		GP	DTP
Basic Facilities
A Bastion can have any number of Basic Facilities.

Special Facilities
You must meet all prerequisites of a Special Facility in order to build and use it. The maximum number of Special Facilities your Bastion can have depends on your character's level (see Appendix B of the TBD Guidelines for details)
A Bastion can't have more than one Special Facility of the same kind unless the facility's description says otherwise.
A Special Facility can be replaced with another Special Facility by paying the gold and DTP to build the new facility, but the full gold cost of the old facility contributes to the new one
Only the Bastion's owner can benefit from its Special Facilities except where explicitly noted otherwise.
Certain Special Facilities have associated Craftable Magic Item Types to be used with the Crafting Magic Item downtime activity

# Loot

Jump to
Rolled Loot		Select Loot			Multisession Loot (Full DMs Only)				Other Loot
T0      T1      T2      T3      T4		T1 Select    T2 Select    T3 Select    T4 Select			T1+    T2+     T3+     T4+      Tier S      Relics of Emergence				Deck of Many Things     Hoard Magic Items      Superior Ship Upgrades     Event Items     Unlisted Official Items      Homebrew Items      Legacy Loot
The listed Base Cost of an item does not mean that the magic item can be purchased or sold. Only items as listed in Equipment can be purchased or sold.     
Event items can only be acquired during the event they were created for. DM Select items can only be awarded by a specific DM and other DMs collaborating with them with their permission.
Name		Source	Type	Tier	Description				Notes / Rage Advice
Rolled Loot
Rolled Loot can be rolled in #treasure-log using /hgenloot [Number of Players] [APL]. A DM can also choose to predetermine rolled loot instead of rolling, with a number of item slots = the number of players.
 For predetermined loot, no more than half the items (rounded down) for a game can be permanents and the maximum item tier is up to the tier for the APL. Trial DMs can only predetermine up to T2 Rolled Loot.
A DM can choose to predetermine some of the items and roll for the remainder.
T0 Items (APL 3+)
DMs can add up to a number of predetermined T0 items to a game up to half the number of players (rounded down), and only one of them can be a permanent.
T0 items don't count against the normal number of item slots for a game.
T0 Consumables (Go to T0 Permanents)
DMs can predetermine up to 4 duplicates of a T0 consumable per item slot (up to 15 for ammunition)

T1 Items (APL 3+)
For APL 17+ games, the DM can add bonus loot by rolling loot at APL 4 and selecting either 1 T1 permanent or up to 2 T2 consumables from the list. (The DM can also choose to predetermine the same instead for either T1 Select Loot or Rolled Loot)
T1 Consumables (Go to T1 Permanents)
DMs can predetermine up to 3 duplicates of a T1 consumable per item slot (up to 20 for ammunition)

T3 Items (APL 11+)
Only Full DMs can predetermine T3 loot.
T3 Consumables (Go to T3 Permanents)
DMs can predetermine up to 3 duplicates of a T3 consumable per item slot (up to 20 for ammunition)

Select Loot
Select Loot can only be predetermined by a DM, not rolled.
 For predetermined loot, no more than half the items (rounded down) for a game can be permanents and the maximum item tier is up to the tier for the APL. Trial DMs can only predetermine up to T2 Select Loot.
A DM can choose to predetermine some Select Loot items and roll for the remainder.
T1 Select Items (APL 3+)
For APL 17+ games, the DM can add bonus loot by rolling loot at APL 4 and selecting either 1 T1 permanent or up to 2 T2 consumables from the list. (The DM can also choose to predetermine the same instead for either T1 Select Loot or Rolled Loot)

Multisession Loot
Multisession loot is predetermined loot awarded after a multisession with a listed minimum APL and number of sessions, but must always be at least 3+ sessions. Only Full DMs can drop multisession loot.
Multisession loot doesn't count against the number of item slots for the session it is awarded in; it is awarded in addition to those items as a reward for the multisession as a whole.
Each type of multisession is on its own separate track: for example, an APL 11+ multisession of 3+ sessions could award both a T2+ and a T3+ item.
Multisession loot cannot be crafted.
T1+ Items (APL 1+)
T1+ items are multisession loot that requires a multisession of 3+ sessions at APL 1+ to award and can only be awarded by Full DMs.
T1+ Consumables (Go to T1+ Permanents)
See the notes below for how many duplicates of a T1+ consumable can be awarded for a APL 1+ multisession of 3+ sessions
At this time, there are no T1+ Consumables.

At this time, there are no T1+ Permanents.

Relics of Emergence
Relics of Emergence are unique homebrew multisession loot that use the Vestiges of Divergence system from Explorers Guide to Wildemount.
Relics of Emergence are evolving items that respond to a specific wielder and that change between 3 states with growing abilities and powers: Dormant, Awakened, and Exalted.
As homebrew items, a Full DM must submit a Rules request detailing the Relic of Emergence for approval, including its statistics for each state and may be asked to give an outline on how it is meant to be acquired and upgraded.
The tier of each state of a homebrew Relic of Emergence is determined collaboratively by the Full DM and the Rules team.
An approved Relic of Emergence follows the normal item distribution rules with one change: they are multisession items that require 3+ sessions to drop or upgrade, which can only be done by Full DMs.
An approved Relic of Emergence can only ever be initially acquired in its Dormant state as part of a multisession. Upgrading a Relic of Emergence into an Awakened and later Exalted state requires separate multisessions by a Full DM.
An acquired Relic of Emergence can be upgraded in a multisession ran by a different Full DM than the one that originally dropped it, provided they have the original Full DM’s consent and access to the original request.
The full statistics of a Relic of Emergence are known only to the original Full DM and the staff teams, as well as any DMs they collaborate with. A player learns the item's statistics only as it reveals more of its power by evolving.
Relics of Emergence are almost always unique: only one of that specific kind of Relic of Emergence will ever exist, as homebrewed by a Full DM and approved by the Lore and Rules teams.

The minimum APL and number of sessions depends on this state's tier, but is always at least 3 sessions.
Other Loot
Deck of Many Things
The Deck of Many Things (DMG2014/2024), the Deck of Many More Things (BMT), and the Deck of Wonder (BMT) cannot be acquired by PCs but a DM can choose to incorporate and use them in an adventure.
Any lasting effects of a Deck are localized to the adventure only and they expire and reverse themselves in a narratively coherent fashion at the end of an adventure.
PCs can find and acquire individual cards of the Deck of Many Things as awarded by DMs. These cards are awarded as Select or Multisession permanent items as listed below.
To use individual cards, the PC must assemble cards they own into a personalized deck after finishing a Long Rest. A personalized deck must contain no duplicates and the maximum deck size is equal to the PC's Proficiency Bonus.
Individual cards can't be used outside of a personalized deck and a PC can only benefit from and use cards from a personalized deck that they make. A PC can only have one personalized deck at a time.
These items cannot be crafted.

Hoard Magic Items
Hoard Magic Items are loot from Fizban’s Treasury of Dragons. Similar to Relics of Emergence, they exist in different states and can grow in power and evolve.
Hoard Magic Items exist in 4 states: Slumbering, Stirring, Wakened, and Ascendant. As described in Fizban's Treasury of Dragons, they can grow in power by one state after being steeped in the hoard of a recently slain dragon.
Each state is associated with a draconic age category: Slumbering for dragons of any age, Stirring for young dragons, Wakened for adult dragons, and Ascendant for ancient dragons.
A Hoard Magic Item steeped in the hoard of a recently slain dragon can't grow beyond the state associated with the dragon's age. For example, steeping a Wakened item in a slain adult dragon's hoard doesn't make it Ascendant.
Hoard Magic Items can be acquired in states higher than Slumbering, as either from a dragon's hoard of appropriate age category or as wielded by others.
Unlike Relics of Emergence, awarding or upgrading Hoard Magic Items follows the normal item distribution rules and can only be awarded or upgraded by Full DMs.
A Hoard Magic Item with the damage resistance Draconic Quirk only grants the benefit of resistance in its Wakened and Ascendant states.
If a character that possesses a Hoard Magic Item trades it or undergoes a checkpoint rework into a different character, the item reverts to its Slumbering state.

Superior Ship Upgrades
Superior Ship Upgrades are upgrades for ships as described in Ghosts of Saltmarsh. Each listed ship upgrade can be awarded as a Select permanent item of the associated tier.
Ship upgrades can only be awarded in a session where at least one or more of the player characters owns an eligible air, space, or water vehicle.
Except where written otherwise, ship upgrades can be installed onto air, space, and water vehicles as listed in Equipment. Ship upgrades cannot be crafted.
Ship upgrades can only be installed while the vehicle is berthed. Installing a ship upgrade costs 20 DTP and 15,000 GP.
Installed ship upgrades cannot be removed or traded. However, if another ship upgrade is installed on the same component of the ship, it replaces any preexisting upgrade on that component.
A ship can only ever have one hull upgrade and one figurehead upgrade respectively. A weapon on a ship can only ever have one upgrade, and a weapon upgrade only affects one weapon on a ship.
A movement component on a ship (such as sails or oars) can only ever have one movement upgrade per movement type (such as sails or oars).
A ship can have any number of miscellaneous upgrades but can only gain a specific miscellaneous upgrade once.

Event Items (Go to Exclusive or to Seasonal)
Unlisted Official Items
Other items that have been officially printed are not present here. There are various reasons why an item might have been omitted, which can include but is not limited to the reasons listed below.
a) The item is unique to an official adventure and has been left to be acquired in that adventure if a DM runs it b) the item is a unique item (such as artifacts) for which multiple copies can't be distributed by multiple DMs c) the item was deemed not to fit well with the server environment as a shared persistent world (such as the Deck of Many Things) or can readily facilitate problematic patterns of gameplay and behavior (such as the Philter of Love)
Name		Source	Type	Tier	Description				Notes / Rage Advice
Official Item Not Listed Here		Varies	Varies	Varies	Item from an official 5e Wizards of the Coast product. See associated book for description.				- A DM must submit a Rules request to drop a specific official item in an adventure. - Bear in mind the reasons listed above. If the item is unique to an official adventure, it will typically require running that adventure to drop. - If the item is a unique item such as an artifact, it will be determined if the item is appropriate for use by a PC in the server's shared persistent world and will be tiered accordingly. Most artifacts will typically be Tier S. - A DM may be asked to provide an outline describing how the item is meant to be acquired.
Homebrew Items
DMs can submit a Rules request to design and award a homebrew magic item of their creation in an adventure.
The design and tier of the item is collaboratively determined by the DM and the Rules team.
Name		Source	Type	Tier	Description				Notes / Rage Advice
Homebrew Item Created by a DM		Approved Homebrew from a DM	Varies	Varies	Homebrew item created by a DM in collaboration with the Rules team.				- The DM should refer to existing 5e magic items to either use as a starting point for their homebrew item or to help inform how the item is designed. - The DM may be asked to provide an outline describing how the item is meant to be acquired. - The item follows the normal distribution rules for items on the server. If the item is determined to be multisession loot of a given tier, the DM must run a multisession of the required minimum APL and number of sessions. - With a DM’s consent, an approved homebrew item can be added as loot to this document to allow that DM and other DMs to award that item without the need for future requests.

Dark Gifts
Dark Gifts are a type of Supernatural Reward that consist of a mixture of beneficial and detrimental abilities due to contact with dark supernatural forces. They cannot be traded or sold.
Dark Gifts do not replace an item in the session. A DM can give a character or multiple characters a Dark Gift at their discretion due to events in an adventure.
A character can only ever have one Dark Gift. If a character receives another Dark Gift, they must choose to keep the old Dark Gift or lose it and replace it with the new one.

Supernatural Gifts
Supernatural Gifts are a type of Supernatural Reward that consist of extraordinary permanent abilities acquired after great deeds or contact with powerful supernatural forces. They cannot be traded or sold.
Supernatural Gifts follow the normal item distribution rules and are treated as permanent items and can only be awarded by Full DMs.
A character can have multiple Supernatural Gifts, but can only benefit from a number whose combined tier is no greater than their Proficiency Bonus. They choose which combination of Supernatural Gifts if any they have active whenever they finish a Long Rest.

For purposes of the number of Supernatural Gifts a character can have active at a time, this Supernatural Gift's tier of play corresponds to the character's tier. For example, it would be treated as a Tier 3 Supernatural Gift when used by a level 12 character (since they gain the benefit of the level 11 trait).
Oracle		MOT	Supernatural Gift (Supernatural Reward)	T1+	Supernatural Gift. See book for description.				- A character can only obtain this Supernatural Gift in relation to a deity or faith as deternined by the DM. When a character gains this Supernatural Gift, their starting Piety score with respect to that deity or faith is 1 unless it is already higher than 1. See Chapter 2 of the Mythic Odysseys of Theros for rules on how this score works (ignore the guidance regarding changing gods).  - As a guideline, a character can expect their Piety to increase by 1 during most sessions of play assuming they are following following the tenets of that deity or faith, and a single deed typically changes their Piety score by only 1 point in either direction unless the action is very significant. - A character's Piety score can change even when this Supernatural Gift is not active. - A DM that awards this Supernatural Gift must note in their session log the deity or faith this Supernatural Gift is associated with, as well as circumstances that can increase or decrease Piety, following the template in Mythic Odysseys of Theros. - Each Piety threshold for this Supernatural Gift also has a level threshold. A character must be level 1+ to benefit from the Augur trait (Piety 3+), must be level 5+ to benefit from the Seer trait (Piety 10+), must be level 11+ to benefit from the Sibyl trait (Piety 25+), and must be level 17+ to benefit from the Divine Oracle trait (Piety 50+). - For purposes of the number of Supernatural Gifts a character can have active at a time, this Supernatural Gift's tier of play corresponds to the tier of the highest level trait they benefit from. For example, it would be treated as a Tier 3 Supernatural Gift when used by a level 12 character with 30 Piety (since they gain the benefit of the Sibyl trait which requires being level 11+ and 25+ Piety).

Mythic Gifts (Tier S)
Mythic Gifts are a type of Supernatural Reward that consist of extraordinary permanent abilities only available to level 20 characters. They cannot be traded or sold.
Mythic Gifts are Tier S rewards: they are multisession rewards that require 8+ sessions at APL 20 to award and can only be awarded by Full DMs.
A character can have multiple Mythic Gifts, but can only benefit from one at a time. They choose which Mythic Gift if any they have active whenever they finish a Long Rest.
Name		Source	Type	Tier	Description				Notes / Rage Advice
Boon of High Magic		DMG2014	Mythic Gift (Supernatural Reward)	Tier S	Mythic Gift. See book for description.
Boon of Spell Recall		DMG2014	Mythic Gift (Supernatural Reward)	Tier S	Mythic Gift. See book for description.
Charms
Charms are a type of Supernatural Reward that consist of minor abilities that typically have limited uses similar to consumable items. They cannot be traded or sold.
Charms follow the normal item distribution rules and are treated as consumable items (only 1 charm per item slot except where otherwise stated)
A character can have any number of charms, but can't benefit from the same charm more than once: a character receiving the same charm again only regains any expended uses of the charm.

If the produced spell requires concentration, it instead lasts for its full duration. If the produced spell has a range of self, you're the target. If the produced spell has a range of self with an area, you're the point of origin. Miscellaneous
Other Miscellaneous Rewards
Jump to
Quirks	Piety
Name		Source	Type	Tier	Description				Notes / Rage Advice
Bastion		DMG2024	Bastion	Varies	A Bastion obtained as part of an adventure.				- DMs can award a Bastion as part of an adventure, which doesn’t count against the adventure’s gold allotment or item slots. The number of Special Facilities and their level prerequisites must adhere to the table in Appendix B of the TBD Guidelines, using the Average Party Level (APL).  - These Bastions can’t be used in downtime or otherwise altered or expanded; however, Bastion Turns can be taken during adventures with a DM’s permission.  - Items crafted and effects gained at these Bastions can’t persist beyond an adventure.  - A character awarded a Bastion in this way can claim it as their new Bastion (which can be used in downtime) by using the rules for Additional Bastions in Appendix B of the TBD Guidelines and expending the full cost in gold and DTP for the awarded Bastion’s facilities. The player must have the original DM’s permission to do so, which must be noted in the session log originally or as later edited.
Quirk		Homebrew by a Full DM	Supernatural Reward	T0	A character gains a minor alteration to their person that is either cosmetic or provides a mechanical benefit no greater than a minor magical property.				- Only Full DMs can create and award Quirks without a request.  - Quirks don't take up item slots for a session and the DM can give a character a Quirk at their discretion due to events in an adventure. - A character can have any number of Quirks. They can't be traded or sold. - The effect of a Quirk should either be cosmetic or if it has a mechanical effect, the effect should be no greater than a minor magical property as described in the 2014/2024 DMG (except for the Guardian or Unbreakable properties)
Homebrew Supernatural Reward (Dark Gift, Supernatural Gift, Mythic Gift, or Charm)		Approved Homebrew by a DM	Supernatural Reward	Varies	Homebrew Supernatural Reward created by a DM in collaboration with the Rules team.				- DMs can submit a Rules request to design and award a homebrew Supernatural Reward of their creation in an adventure. - The design and tier of the Supernatural Reward is collaboratively determined by the DM and the Rules team.         - The DM should refer to existing 5e Supernatural Rewards to either use as a starting point for their homebrew reward or to help inform how the reward is designed. - The DM may be asked to  provide an outline describing how the reward is meant to be acquired. - The reward follows the normal distribution rules for items on the server. If the item is determined to be multisession loot of a given tier, the DM must run a multisession of the required minimum APL and number of sessions. - With a DM’s consent, an approved homebrew Supernatural Reward can be added to this document to allow that DM and other DMs to award that reward without the need for future requests.
Piety		Approved Homebrew by a Full DM	Supernatural Gift (Supernatural Reward)	T1+	Homebrew Supernatural Gift created by a Full DM in collaboration with the Lore and Rules teams, using the Piety system as detailed in Mythic Odysseys of Theros.				- A character with this kind of Supernatural Gift has a starting Piety score of 1 in relation to a deity or faith, unless it is already higher than 1. See Chapter 2 of the Mythic Odysseys of Theros for rules on how this score works (ignore the guidance regarding changing gods).  - As a guideline, a character can expect their Piety to increase by 1 during most sessions of play assuming they are following following that deity or faith's tenets, and a single deed typically changes their Piety score by only 1 point in either direction unless the action is very significant. - A DM that awards this Supernatural Gift must note in their session log the deity or faith this Supernatural Gift is associated with, as well as circumstances that can increase or decrease Piety, following the template in Mythic Odysseys of Theros. - A character's Piety score can change even when this Supernatural Gift is not active. - Each Piety Supernatural Gift provides certain benefits at certain Piety thresholds—3, 10, 25, and 50—which have associated level prerequisites as well: level 1+, 5+, 11+, and 17+ respectively. - For purposes of the number of Supernatural Gifts a character can have active at a time, this Supernatural Gift's tier of play corresponds to the tier of the highest level trait they benefit from. For example, it would be treated as a Tier 3 Supernatural Gift when used by a level 12 character with 30 Piety (since they gain the benefit of the trait that requires being level 11+ and having 25+ Piety). - The design and approval of this Supernatural Gift follows the process described above for Homebrew Supernatural Rewards.
Quirks (Go to Other Miscellaneous Rewards)
Quirks don't take up an item slot and can only be awarded by Full DMs

Quirk		Homebrew by a Full DM	Supernatural Reward	T0	A character gains a minor alteration to their person that is either cosmetic or provides a mechanical benefit no greater than a minor magical property.				- Only Full DMs can create and award Quirks without a request.  - Quirks don't take up item slots for a session and the DM can give a character a Quirk at their discretion due to events in an adventure. - A character can have any number of Quirks. They can't be traded or sold. - The effect of a Quirk should either be cosmetic or if it has a mechanical effect, the effect should be no greater than a minor magical property as described in the 2014/2024 DMG (except for the Guardian or Unbreakable properties) - A list of available Quirks can also be seen below that are available to any DM to award without a request.

General Information (Go to Piety)
Name		Source	Type	Tier	Description				Notes / Rage Advice
Piety		Approved Homebrew by a Full DM	Supernatural Gift (Supernatural Reward)	T1+	Homebrew Supernatural Gift created by a Full DM in collaboration with the Lore and Rules teams, using the Piety system as detailed in Mythic Odysseys of Theros.				- A character with this kind of Supernatural Gift has a starting Piety score of 1 in relation to a deity or faith unless it is already higher than 1. See Chapter 2 of the Mythic Odysseys of Theros for rules on how this score works (ignore the guidance regarding changing gods).  - As a guideline, a character can expect their Piety to increase by 1 during most sessions of play assuming they are following following the tenets of that deity or faith, and a single deed typically changes their Piety score by only 1 point in either direction unless the action is very significant. - A DM that awards this Supernatural Gift must note in their session log the deity or faith this Supernatural Gift is associated with, as well as circumstances that can increase or decrease Piety, following the template in Mythic Odysseys of Theros. - A character's Piety score can change even when this Supernatural Gift is not active. - Each Piety Supernatural Gift provides certain benefits at certain Piety thresholds—3, 10, 25, and 50—which have associated level prerequisites as well: level 1+, 5+, 11+, and 17+ respectively. - For purposes of the number of Supernatural Gifts a character can have active at a time, this Supernatural Gift's tier of play corresponds to the tier of the highest level trait they benefit from. For example, it would be treated as a Tier 3 Supernatural Gift when used by a level 12 character with 30 Piety (since they gain the benefit of the trait that requires being level 11+ and having 25+ Piety). - The design and approval of this Supernatural Gift follows the process described above for Homebrew Supernatural Rewards. - A list of available Piety Supernatural Gifts can also be seen below that are available to any Full DM to award without a request, each associated with a specific deity or faith.

Mounts
A mount is a creature that can be used by a PC as a mount following the rules for mounted combat.
The mounts listed below follow the normal item distribution rules and are treated as permanent items (only 1 mount per item slot)
Sapient mounts such as nightmares or unicorns cannot be traded and they can only be used as controlled mounts at a DM's discretion.
A sapient mount's name, alignment, and personality must be written in the session log in which it is acquired.
A DM can use the guidelines on customizing monsters in the DM Guidelines for mounts they award.
A DM can choose to award a mount that has been awakened (such as by the Awaken spell) but the mount is then considered to be a sapient mount.

Pets
Pets can't take the Attack or Help actions and normally can't participate in adventures: a pet only participates in adventures otherwise with a DM's express permission and at their discretion
A DM can submit a Rules request to award a creature not listed here as a vanity pet
Pets can also be awarded as the equivalent of a T0 Permanent item and follow the associated item distribution rules for doing so 
A DM can use the guidelines on customizing monsters in the DM Guidelines for pets they award.
Sapient pets cannot be traded or sold.                                                
A sapient pet's name, alignment, and personality must be written in the session log in which it is acquired.
A DM can choose to award a pet that has been awakened (such as by the Awaken spell) but the mount is then considered to be a sapient pet.
Name		Source	Type	Tier	Description				Notes / Rage Advice
Pets listed in Equipment		Varies	Pet	T0	Pets as listed in Equipment
Renown
At character creation, a character can start with a Renown score of 3 as a respected member of a group in the setting. If the group is listed below, they start with any associated Renown Perks
If the chosen group requires collaborating with a specific DM, the player first needs that DM's permission to be a member of the group at character creation.
